戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 短期地緣風險降溫: 川普取消大規模襲擊伊朗, 貝森特釋出談判消息訊號, 帶動市場風險偏好改善。油
價與美債利率同步回落, 市場情緒回穩並推動股市反彈。
• 中長天期利率偏高, 反映期限溢價上升: 除了美國財政紀律存疑, 市場亦在適應Warsh的新風格: 關注
Fed對通膨風險的判斷是否具公信力, 近期亦傳出Warsh考慮減少FOMC開會次數。
• 美日聯合干預, 日圓回升助力亞幣: 日圓自低點反彈, 惟美日利差仍大, 單靠官方介入難扭轉偏貶態勢。
• 去槓桿壓力緩解, 獲利面支撐中長期趨勢: 前波市場修正使部分高槓桿籌碼出場, 近期股市有所反彈。
短期需留意部分擁擠交易、籌碼調整仍有壓力, 且市場對AI獲利期待已偏高; AI前景仍支撐長線多頭。
2. 後續看點:
• 美伊談判進展, 7月美國CPI(8/12), FOMC會議紀錄(8/20)。
策略思維
1) 股市−獲利仍是中長期主導因素: 市場情緒帶動科技股反彈, 短期仍須留意漲多/高評價個股波動。然而, AI需

求支撐企業獲利前景, 有助長線多頭格局。
2) 債市−長端利率下行風險有限: 油價降溫提振利率曲線回落, 但期限溢價、美債供給壓力、聯準會未來QT方
向, 將限縮長天期利率下行空間。
3) 匯市−日圓驅動亞幣反彈, 但尚難形成趨勢性扭轉: 日圓回升改善亞洲資金流向與風險偏好, 惟美元仍受高利
環境支撐, 且地緣風險並未完全消散, 亞幣升值動能仍需觀察。

油價隨中東消息震盪, 不同情境對油價年增率影響 川普TACO指數(註)
地緣政治｜川普稱將與伊朗談判, 未對達成協議設期限, 油價應聲回落
• 中東最新局勢: 川普與財政部長貝森特表示正在與伊朗談判, 協議可能「在近期達成」, 海峽「很快就會開放」; 伊
方副外長則表示, 美方「準備重返承諾」(6月MOU), 但強調路徑是伊朗與阿曼協商, 非美伊直接談判。核計畫限制、
全面制裁解除、凍結資產釋放、黎巴嫩停火落實, 以及海峽管理權(伊朗收費與否)仍是核心分歧。
• 觀察近期川普TACO指數(Trump Always Chickens Out Index)明顯回升, 反映金融市場所累積的壓力已達相對高位,
這也印證週末川普態度為何急轉直下, 設法促成協議。
• 展望後市, 美方要求讓荷姆茲海峽能「立即、徹底且全面的」重新開放, 並「終結伊朗的核子威脅」, 但德黑蘭必
須「迅速達成協議」; 但鑑於雙方立場迥異, 過程中消息面仍主導油價走勢, 短線油價仍隨新聞上沖下洗。

政策不具限制性。
• 7月FOMC會議後, 多數官員仍維持謹慎偏鷹立場。其中, 三位於7月會議投下反對票的官員相繼發表聲明, 偏好透
過適度升息 以降低未來更大幅度緊縮的風險 ; 而本週多名官員接續演講 , 儘管並非所有官員認定目前必須升息
(Williams、Paulson偏中性), 但委員們對通膨風險的警戒仍高, 亦支撐市場升息預期高檔。
• 另一方面, 《紐約時報》報導指出, 主席Warsh於7月會議曾提出減少例行會議次數, 此舉與Warsh近來主張淡化政
策溝通、提升政策彈性的方向一致。據傳Warsh提出方案為將每年例行會議由8次調整6次, 並另外安排2場經濟議
題會議(不談論貨幣政策)。Fed政策透明度及前瞻指引功能恐進一步下降, 市場亦將重新評估Fed決策框架。

油價震盪牽動利率降溫, 惟中長天期利率仍處高位 投機部位仍看空10年美國公債
利率｜油價與2年債利率同步回落, 長天期利率仍維持高檔
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8/7。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/7/28。
• 美債利率隨同油價同步回落, 隨財長貝森特稱「可能達成協議、開放荷姆茲海峽」的言論, 帶動WTI跌破80美元。
近期2年債利率回落幅度較大, 但中長天期利率仍處高位, 其一原因為市場對Fed政策公信力、獨立性仍有顧慮。儘
管Warsh認為長端利率上行已形成部分緊縮效果, 但市場擔憂若持續視其為不升息的理由, 並隨目前美國經濟表現
強勁、美伊前景存在不確定下, 影響利率高檔震盪。
• 籌碼面而言, CFTC投機部位(非商業)仍維持公債淨空單部位, 顯示市場對中長端利率仍抱持警戒態度。在資金流向
尚未明顯改善下, 中長天期利率偏向上行的壓力仍大。

美國財政部上修Q3借款規模 財政赤字與淨發債量同步維持高檔
資料來源: Bloomberg, Sifma, 截至2026/6/30。
美債｜財政部上調Q3借款需求, 近一年淨發債達2兆美元
註: 單位為億美元。
資料來源: 美國財政部預估。
• 最新季度借款預估顯示, Q2實際淨發債需求1900億美元, 與5月預估大致相符, 季末TGA餘額9190億元亦達標。另
方面, 財政部將Q3借款需求上調680億至7390億美元, 主因政府現金流入低於先前預期, 反映關稅退稅、軍費開支、
減稅效應。
• 中長期發債壓力值得關注: 據TBAC(財政部諮詢委員會)會議資料顯示, 部分一級交易商認為, 未來融資需求恐逐步
超出現行發債框架可支應的規模, 反映後續仍有提高淨發債量的可能性; 目前近一年美國財政赤字、淨發行量均近
2兆美元。考量Fed未來QT減少流動性供給, 導致長天期利率維持高檔, 短期內增發長債的可能性有限。

• 自7/30起, 美日多次進行匯率干預使日圓急升, 此次干預的主因可歸咎於孱弱的日圓將導致輸入性通膨, 雖目前日
本的CPI尚低, 但未來的通膨壓力已然浮現。日本央行預估2026、2027財年的通膨將持續高於2%的目標。

• 然而要使日圓扭轉長期貶勢並不容易, 除了官方干預外, 還需要三大關鍵因素, 一是需要聯準會貨幣政策明確轉向,
二是油價回落使輸入性通膨壓力緩解, 三是日本央行升息的路徑、幅度與終端利率等態度更趨明確。

製造業景氣與企業獲利同步增溫 標普500 Q2獲利明顯上修
美股｜製造業景氣逐步升溫, 支持企業獲利動能上修
• 美國7月ISM製造業指數升至55.6, 不僅優於市場預期, 更創下近4年新高。其中, 新訂單、產出及就業指數同步回
升, 顯示人工智慧、半導體及國防需求持續支撐製造業景氣。不過, 傳統產業如金屬、運輸、化學及消費相關產業,
仍面臨需求疲弱、關稅成本攀升、地緣政治風險及定價混亂等多重壓力。
• 截至7/31, 已有61%的S&P 500企業公布第二季財報, 整體獲利年增率達47.4%, 創2021年以來最強勁的成長動能。
其中, 86%的企業EPS優於市場預期, 高於近五年平均的78%。亞馬遜獲利大幅優於預期, 帶動非必需消費品產業
獲利年增率達90%, 谷歌獲利表現亮眼, 亦推升通訊服務產業獲利年增率至17.9%。不過, 吉利德與默克因併購認
列無形資產減損, 導致醫療保健產業獲利下滑。整體而言, AI需求持續強勁, 帶動科技與通訊服務產業獲利顯著成

長, 並進一步推升整體企業獲利動能。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 標普500截至2026Q2、ISM製造業截至2026/7。 資料來源: Bloomberg, Factset。前次預估為2026/6/30, 最新為2026/7/31。

AI需求強勁, 帶動AMD營收動能走強 CSP業者的資本支出上升, 支撐供應鏈營收持續升溫
科技｜AI需求趨勢不變, 半導體的拉回仍視為分批進場契機
• AMD公布Q2財報, 受惠於資料中心需求大幅成長, 帶動整體營收年增50%至115億美元, 創下今年以來最強勁的成
長動能。同時, EPS達1.38美元, 創下歷史新高。展望下半年, 公司預估Q3營收將再創新高, 達130億美元, 毛利率
亦可望持續攀升, 主因在於伺服器CPU市占率持續提升, 加上AI GPU開始放量出貨, 帶動產品組合持續改善。
• 自CSP陸續公布財報以來, 雖然自由現金流轉負一度引發市場疑慮 , 但並未改變業者對AI需求強勁的看法。因此,
各大CSP持續上調今年資本支出, 五大CSP業者今年合計資本支出挑戰1兆美元。整體而言, AI需求趨勢並未改變,
半導體指數近期的拉回, 仍可視為分批布局的契機。
資料來源: Bloomberg, AMD Q2財報。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 投行預估2026年預估值。

7/31大漲日融資反而大幅減少 7/31大漲日外資買超(散戶賣超)創歷史紀錄
韓股｜底部出現明顯籌碼換手, 韓國為全球股市帶來的動盪有望落下帷幕
• 7/31亞洲股市全面報復性反彈, 韓國股市單日上漲近18%。但籌碼面出現有趣的現象, 當日韓股融資餘額不增反而
大減超過3兆韓元, 同時當日外資買超與散戶賣超均創下紀錄, 顯示散戶有明顯「投降」行為, 底部出現大規模籌碼
換手為正向訊號, 反映市場主導力量由散戶槓桿資金轉向機構投資人。
• 綜上所述, 未來韓國股市的波動有望逐漸降低, 同時走勢將慢慢回歸基本面主導而非情緒面驅動, 也對於其他亞洲
股市與全球科技股的市場氛圍也有正向幫助。
資料來源: KOFIA, 截至2026/8/4。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8/4。

台股歷經多次融資大幅清洗, 但指數跌幅往往有限 台積電營收預估將保持強勁成長到2028年
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8/5。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 預估範圍:2026~2028年。
• 融資遭清洗、斷頭並非罕有, 當投資人提高槓桿時, 若股價突然重挫, 容易引發融資斷頭, 並進一步造成在高點追價
的融資多殺多, 造成台股融資減幅普遍高於指數跌幅, 融資餘額跌幅約是指數跌幅的2倍以上。不過, 融資餘額大幅
去化後, 股市能否反彈仍視基本面是否正向, 若股市基本面仍正向, 融資去化後將再次累積向上。
• AI需求供不應求、台積電大舉提高資本支出, 將顯著提高台積電高效能運算的營收佔比, 成為未來營收高成長引擎。
高效能運算營收佔比從2023年的43%, 預估今年將升至63%, 在AI需求帶動下, 未來將朝70%佔比前進, 加速台積電

營收成長, 預估明、後年台積電總營收增速有望達34%、25%以上。
台股｜融資大幅去化後, 股市走勢回歸基本面; 台積電營收前景強勁, 支持股市長期多頭

第二季GDP季增1.5%, 預估下半年持續增長 7月製造業、服務業信心回升
註: Q2 GDP為季增年率, 細項為GDP增速貢獻度。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q2。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/7。
美國景氣｜內需、投資支撐第二季GDP; ISM反映企業信心升溫
• 美國第二季GDP季增年率1.5%, 低於市場預期, 主要受淨出口貢獻-1.01%、企業去庫存-0.67%拖累。所幸, 占經
濟活動逾三分之二的民間消費季增3.2%, 顯示內需仍具韌性; 惟需留意, 若下半年中東戰事導致美國汽油價格維持
震盪, 恐影響消費意願。另方面, 非住宅固定投資成長8.4%, 企業投資動能穩健, 其中AI相關資本支出為重要支撐。
• 7月ISM調查顯示景氣動能改善: 1)製造業PMI升至55.6, 創4年多來新高; 新訂單、生產與就業指數同步走揚, 其中
生產指數躍升至58.5, 反映企業積極擴充產能。2)服務業PMI小幅上升至54.1, 連續25個月處於擴張區間, 企業、消

費者需求持續支撐經濟動能。惟需留意, 價格指數均偏高: 製造業(71.1)、服務業(70.3), 通膨壓力仍未完全消退。

ADP不如預期, 商品製造業轉負 JOLTS: 勞動市場維持均衡, 僱用與流出大致持平
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/7。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/7。
• ADP: 7月新增就業人數4.4萬, 動能創今年一月以來新低。服務業(+4.7萬)主要仍由教育與醫療貢獻, 休閒與餐飲人
數減少, 反映季節活動支撐效應退去; 商品製造(-3千)疲軟, 受天然資源及礦業拖累。
• JOLTS: 6月職缺數下滑至735.9萬人, 低於市場預估; 職缺/失業比為1.04, 整體供需大致均衡。以細項來看, 僱用率
(Hires)與勞動力流出(Separations)維持相近水準, 反映勞動流動狀況平穩; 勞動流出(Separations)方面, 裁員率與
自主離職率維持偏低, 顯示企業尚未明顯縮減人力, 但勞工轉職意願亦有限。
• 美國勞動市場供需大致維持平衡狀態, 雖然新增就業動能放緩, 但仍維持穩健擴張格局, 亦未出現明顯惡化情勢。

%%
All (左軸)
Service (左軸)
%
存款利率 (右軸)
• ECB最新薪資追蹤顯示, 市場擔心的「薪資推升第二波通膨」並未出現。歐元區協商薪資增幅已由2025年的3%以
上降至2026年的2.3%至2.6%, 且不論是否納入一次性通膨補貼, 各項薪資指標都逐步收斂。這代表能源危機期間

的特殊補償措施正在退場, 薪資成長回歸正常軌道, 未來數季薪資壓力大致可控。
• 然而, 歐元區7月整體通膨率回升至2.9%, 服務業通膨更由3.2%升至3.3%, 顯示企業仍透過旅遊、餐飲、交通及保
險等項目轉嫁成本。市場或許因此擔憂ECB仍有升息必要, 但從薪資降溫趨勢來看, 通膨壓力並未進一步擴散, 服
務業價格黏著性也許較偏向落後反映先前成本上升 。ECB短時間缺乏重新升息的條件 , 按兵不動仍是最可能的政
策選擇。

中國GDP增長放緩, 使7月政治局會議政治需提出因應 反應中央要求靠前發力, 發債落點已較為提前
中國｜7月中共中央政治局會議, 下半年宏觀調控定調為「發力提效」, 為中港股有利支撐
• 上半年GDP增速放緩至4.7%, 消費疲弱。對比去年12月、今年4月會議, 仍稱「做優增量、盤活存量, 加大逆週期
和跨週期調節力度」, 7月會議則稱「加快實施好更加積極的財政政策和適度寬鬆的貨幣政策 , 充分發揮各項存量
政策效能, 及時謀劃出台務實管用的增量政策, 加大逆週期調節力度」。顯示中共中央對情勢研判有所轉變, 尤其
未提“跨週期”凸顯官方要表達更加重視當下局勢, 下半年政策將更加寬鬆, 而原本已“靠前發力”的發債將更被落實,
政策方向則在推進六張網與人工智慧方面 。另, 決定10月召開五中全會, 凸顯2026年地方換屆與2027年二十一大

人事布局是同一政治週期的不同階段, 把“全面從嚴治黨”作為主要議程, 則凸顯長期黨建路線與組織淨化的延續。
• 展望後市,基於: 1)政策態度更為放鬆; 2)晶片自給、國有化供應鏈發展故事不變。因此, AI趨勢不變, AI概念股拉回
仍視為布局機會。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q2。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/6。

市, 推升以2年債為首的日本公債殖利率上行, 也使得日銀升息機率增加, 升息預期雖然對股市形成評價面壓力, 但
利率走高對於銀行業獲利則是正向。

美債殖利率小幅下降, 緩解亞幣貶值壓力 日圓與其他亞幣普遍由貶轉升

• 美日聯手干預日圓後, 日圓獨強後震盪, 並未帶動亞幣同步大幅升值, 反映短期干預對日圓以外的貨幣影響有限。

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

7 月通胀点评

需求支撑，波动放大   
7 月 CPI 同比增速和 PPI 同比增速均受国际油价波动影响较大；但需求端对
物价的支撑也较明显，CPI 中服务消费季节性走强，扣除能源的工业消费品
对 CPI 同比增速上拉也较明显，PPI 中产业转型升级和消费提质扩容对相关
行业产品价格带动也较明显；AI 科技发展的影响已经开始体现在物价当中，
CPI 中的消费电子产品和 PPI 中的计算机通信行业产品价格都表现出对通胀
的上拉影响。 
 7 月 CPI 环比下降 0.1%，同比增长 0.5%，核心 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，服务
价格同比增长 0.7%，消费品价格同比增长 0.2%。 
 从环比看，7 月国际市场价格波动影响国内汽油价格环比下降 10.7%，影
响 CPI 环比下降约 0.35 个百分点 ； 人工智能推动消费电子产品迭代升级 ，
平板电脑 、 计算机和移动电话机合计影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点 ；
暑期出行需求增加，旅行社收费、宾馆住宿、飞机票、交通工具租赁合
计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.10 个百分点；部分地区政策性调价持续推进，
医疗服务影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。 
 CPI 同比涨幅比上月回落 0.5 个百分点，主要是受汽油价格涨幅回落影
响。7 月从同比看，猪肉价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.25 个百分点，汽油
价格对 CPI 的上拉影响比上月减少约 0.45 个百分点，扣除能源的工业消
费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.37 个百分点，服务价格影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.36 个百分点，对 CPI 同比增速拉动较大的是扣除能源的工业消费
品和服务。我们认为年内 CPI 同比增速将维持同比正增长，但同比增速
或在 0.5%-1.0%区间波动，与此同时建议关注以下方面：一是考虑到中东
地缘局势的不稳定性，国际能源价格波动性仍在较高水平，可能对 CPI
月度之间的波动影响较大，二是服务价格波动，更多反映的是季节性消
费特点，目前尚未表现出趋势性走强，三是 AI 科技发展推动的工业消费
品价格合计影响 7 月 CPI 同比上涨约 0.14 个百分点，四是 7 月食品价格
环比增速弱于季节性，但是猪肉价格环比由降转涨，需要关注猪肉价格
环比上涨的持续性和对 CPI 的持续影响。 
 7 月 PPI 环比下降 0.7%，同比增长 3.5%，其中生产资料同比增长 4.8%，
生活资料同比下降 0.8%。7 月 PPIRM 环比下降 1.0%，同比增长 5.5%。 
 7 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点 ： 一是输入性因素影响国内相关行业价格下
行，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造、有色金属矿采
选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比下降约 0.65
个百分点。二是季节性因素影响部分行业价格下降，黑色金属 冶炼和压
延加工业、非金属矿物制品业、水力发电、风力发电，4 个行业合计影响
PPI 环比下降约 0.11 个百分点 。 三是产业转型升级和消费提质扩容带动部
分行业需求增加、价格上涨。 
 PPI 波动预期加大。从行业角度看，拉动 PPI 上行的 9 个行业在 7 月的上
拉影响减少，是 PPI 同比增速低于预期的主要原因，其中，能源类的石油
天然气和化工产品价格，主要受国际能源价格波动和国内季节性需求波
动的影响，煤炭价格主要受季节性需求变动因素影响，目前来看受需求
较强影响导致价格走强的主要是有色金属、计算机通信和电气设备。我
们认为需求支撑和波动影响将主导下半年 PPI 同比增速变化 ， 我们维持此
前观点，认为 2026 年下半年 PPI 同比增速将维持相对较强的走势，不排
除部分月份同比增速上行突破 4.0%。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 8 月 10 日 7 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
7 月 CPI 环比第三个月回落。7 月 CPI 环比下降 0.1%，增速较 6 月上升 0.2 个百分点。从分项环比
看，7 月环比上行的包括教育文化娱乐（1.0%）、医疗保健（0.8%）、生活用品及服务（0.4%），
环比增速下降的有衣着（ -0.4%）和交通通信（-2.2%），环比持平的是食品烟酒、居住和其他用品
和服务；7 月分项中较 6 月环比增速上升的有其他用品和服务、教育文化娱乐、生活用品及服务、
医疗保健、食品烟酒，环比增速较 6 月下降的是衣着、交通通信。 
7 月 CPI 同比增速低于万得一致预期，增速连续两个月回落。7 月 CPI 同比增长 0.5%，较 6 月下降
0.5 个百分点，核心 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较 6 月下降 0.1 个百分点，服务价格同比增长 0.7%，较 6
月下降 0.1 个百分点，消费品价格同比增长 0.2%，较 6 月下降 0.9 个百分点。从分项同比看，7 月同
比增长的是其他用品和服务 （5.8%） 、医疗保健 （2.9%） 、衣着 （1.4%） 、教育文化娱乐 （1.3%）、
生活用品及服务 （0.8%） 和交通通信 （0.4%） ， 同比增速下降的是居住 （-0.3%） 和食品烟酒 （-0.8%），
同比增速较 6 月上升的仅有医疗保健，同比增速较 6 月下降的有教育文化娱乐、生活用品及服务、
其他用品和服务和交通通信。 
7 月食品价格延续分化格局。从环比看，7 月食品价格与上月持平，低于季节性水平 0.6 个百分点；
其中，鲜菜价格环比上涨 1.3%，鸡蛋价格环比下降 2.1%，均明显低于季节性水平；应季水果大量上
市，市场供应充足，鲜果价格环比下降3.8%，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.07 个百分点；生猪产能综合调
控政策效应显现，叠加部分地区高温、强降雨等极端天气频发推高运输成本等因素，猪肉价格由上
月环比下降 0.8%转为环比上涨 4.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。从同比看，7 月猪肉价
格同比下降 13.3%，降幅比上月收窄 2.6 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.25 个百分点。 
能源价格影响较大。从环比看，7 月国际市场价格波动影响国内汽油价格环比下降 10.7%，降幅比上
月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.35 个百分点；人工智能推动消费电子产品迭代升级，
相关产品需求增加、价格上涨，平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格分别环比上涨 11.3%、5.5%和
1.0%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点；服务价格由上月持平转为环比上涨 0.4%，影响 CPI
环比上涨约 0.21 个百分点；服务中，暑期出行需求增加，旅行社收费、宾馆住宿、飞机票、交通工
具租赁价格分别环比上涨 7.2%、6.5%、4.2%和 3.6%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.10 个百分点；部
分地区政策性调价持续推进，医疗服务价格环比上涨 1.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。从
同比看，7 月汽油价格同比上涨 1.0%，涨幅比上月回落 16.0 个百分点，对 CPI 的上拉影响比上月减
少约 0.45 个百分点 ， 带动能源价格同比涨幅回落至0.6%； 扣除能源的工业消费品价格同比上涨1.5%，
涨幅比上月回落 0.2 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.37 个百分点；其中，黄金饰品、个人护理用
品和家用器具价格分别同比上涨 24.6%、1.7%和 0.2%， 涨幅均有回落 ， 合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.13
个百分点；计算机、平板电脑和移动电话机价格分别同比上涨 17.4%、17.2%和 8.5%，涨幅均有扩
大，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.14 个百分点；服务价格同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分
点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.36 个百分点；服务中，医疗服务价格同比上涨 4.3%，涨幅比上月扩大
0.9 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.28 个百分点；家政服务、在外餐饮和教育服务价格分别同比
上涨 1.3%、1.0%和 0.6%，涨幅总体稳定。 
CPI 同比涨幅比上月回落 0.5 个百分点，主要是受汽油价格涨幅回落影响。7 月从同比看，猪肉价格
影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.25 个百分点 ， 汽油价格对CPI 的上拉影响比上月减少约 0.45 个百分点，扣除
能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.37 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.36 个百
分点，对 CPI 同比增速拉动较大的是扣除能源的工业消费品和服务 。我们认为年内 CPI 同比增速将
维持同比正增长，但同比增速或在 0.5%-1.0%区间波动，与此同时建议关注以下方面：一是 考虑到
中东地缘局势的不稳定性，国际能源价格波动性仍在较高水平，可能对 CPI 月度之间的波动影响较
大，二是服务价格波动，更多反映的是季节性消费特点，目前尚未表现出趋势性走强，三是AI 科技
发展推动的工业消费品价格合计影响 7 月 CPI 同比上涨约 0.14 个百分点，四是 7 月食品价格环比增
速弱于季节性，但是猪肉价格环比由降转涨，需要关注猪肉价格环比上涨的持续性和对 CPI 的持续
影响。

### Page 4

2026 年 8 月 10 日 7 月通胀点评 4

PPI 
PPI 环比第二个月负增长。7 月 PPI 环比下降 0.7%，较 6 月下降 0.4 个百分点，其中生产资料环比
下降 0.9%，生活资料环比下降 0.1%。7 月 PPI 同比增长 3.5%，较 6 月下降 0.6 个百分点，其中生产
资料同比增长 4.8%，较6 月下降 0.7 个百分点 ， 生活资料同比下降0.8%，较6 月上升 0.1 个百分点。 
7 月 PPIRM 环比下降 1.0%，同比增长 5.5%，较 6 月下降 0.9 个百分点；从构成来看，7 月同比增速
上升较多的是有色金属材料类（ 19.0%），燃料、动力类（ 9.3%）和化工原料类（9.3%），同比增
速下降的是建筑材料类（-4.1%），农副产品类（-0.8%）和木材及纸浆类（-0.5%）；所有品类中，
同比增速较 6 月上升较多的是建筑材料类，木材及纸浆类和农副产品类，同比增速下降较多的是有
色金属材料类，燃料、动力类和化工原料类。 
7 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是 ， 煤炭开采和洗选业 （27.1%） ， 有色金属矿采选业 （22.6%）
和有色金属冶炼及压延加工业（20.2%），同比增速较低的是酒、饮料和精制茶制造业（ -5.7%），
电力、热力的生产和供应业（-4.0%）和医药制造业（-4.0%）；同比增速较 6 月上升较多的是煤炭
开采和洗选业，造纸及纸制品业和计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业，下降较多的是石油和天然
气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业和有色金属冶炼及压延加工业 。 
输入性因素和季节性因素对 PPI 同比增速拖累较大。7 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点：一是输入性因
素影响国内相关行业价格下行，石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造、有色金属矿采
选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比下降约 0.65 个百分点。二是季节性
因素影响部分行业价格下降，7 月份高温、雨水及台风天气较多，建筑项目施工进度放缓，黑色金
属冶炼和压延加工业、非金属矿物制品业、水力发电、风力发电， 4 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比下降
约 0.11 个百分点。三是产业转型升级和消费提质扩容带动部分行业需求增加、价格上涨，如人工智
能、高端装备、新材料、智能无人飞行器制造、碳素新材料、船舶及相关装置制造、智能家庭消费
设备、护肤用化妆品制造等。 从同比看，价格上涨的主要行业中，石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭
及其他燃料加工业 、 化学原料和化学制品制造业 、 有色金属矿采选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、
黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，6 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约 2.55 个百分点 ； 煤炭开采和洗选业、
电气机械和器材制造业 、 计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业 ，3 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约 1.53
个百分点；上述 9 个行业对 PPI 同比的上拉影响较上月减少 0.56 个百分点。价格同比下拉影响最大
的 5 个行业为：电力热力生产和供应业、汽车制造业、非金属矿物制品业、医药制造业、酒饮料和
精制茶制造业，合计影响 PPI 同比下降约 0.76 个百分点，较上月减少 0.05 个百分点。 
PPI 波动预期加大。从行业角度看，拉动 PPI 同比上行的 9 个行业在 7 月的上拉影响减少，是 PPI
同比增速低于预期的主要原因，其中，能源类的石油天然气和化工产品价格，主要受国际能源价格
波动和国内季节性需求波动的影响，煤炭价格主要受季节性需求变动因素影响，目前来看受需求较
强影响导致价格走强的主要是有色金属、计算机通信和 电气设备。我们认为需求支撑和波动影响将
主导下半年 PPI 同比增速变化，我们维持此前观点，认为 2026 年下半年 PPI 同比增速将维持相对较
强的走势，不排除部分月份同比增速上行突破 4.0%。

披露声明 
本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

Document 265: Capital Market Outlook (2026-07-27)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Moderate Growth, Anchored Inflation, Resilient Risk Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Incoming data suggest U.S. growth remains moderate and close to potential rather than
overheating. Activity is being pulled in both directions: wealth effects, inflation-indexed
government transfers, government spending, tax incentives and the AI investment boom

continue to support it—while higher oil prices and tariffs, slower wage growth, cautious
bank lending, elevated consumer borrowing costs and soft hiring restrain it.
The Federal Reserve’s (Fed) Beige Book prepared for the July 28–29 rate-setting meeting,
supports this view. Over the mid-May to early July reference period, 11 of 12 Fed districts
reported expansion, the broadest improvement since early 2025. Yet, the expansion was
characterized as slight to moderate, with moderate price increases, wage growth modest

to moderate, and employment rising on balance but showing little to no change in seven
of 12 districts. Recent ADP data also show that four-week average employment gains
have started to fade through the July 4 week after picking up earlier this year.
With slower and narrower job creation (private-sector employment outside healthcare
and education is still lower than two years ago), labor-market slack has grown. The
average duration of unemployment has increased, job openings have declined and the

pool of people outside the labor force who nevertheless want a job has expanded. The
lack of labor-market dynamism—partly due to a widening mismatch between jobs and
skills as the economy changes and technology advances—has also restrained wage
growth, constraining consumer spending.
Indeed, although June retail sales surprised to the upside, the underlying signal was less
upbeat than the headline suggested. According to BofA Global Research, non-store retailers

accounted for much of the strength, helped by special promotions that could partly
reverse in July. Spending in most other categories remained tepid, with limited evidence
of a meaningful net boost from the World Cup. While upward revisions to prior months,
especially to food services, lifted expectations for Q2 real consumer spending after a very
weak Q1 gain, real gross domestic product (GDP) still tracks around 1.75% to 2% for Q2,
according to various estimates, consistent with moderate, non-inflationary growth outside

energy-price effects. In fact, renewed fuel-price pressures, alongside fading support from
early-year tax refunds, point to persistent growth headwinds in the second half.
Manufacturing tells a similarly mixed story. After two strong monthly gains earlier in
the year, output increased modestly in May and contracted in June (Exhibit 1A). The AI
investment boom is leaking into large imports of semiconductors and related equipment,
limiting its domestic growth and employment impact relative to headline investment

outlays. Also, a strong dollar restrains exports. On the other hand, lean inventories across
several industries point to a likely restocking cycle in coming quarters. In addition,
defense spending and rebounding aerospace production, alongside favorable regional
Fed July surveys point to continued manufacturing-sector expansion. This is consistent
with further improvement in the Institute for Supply Management manufacturing index—
typically a constructive signal for corporate earnings and investor risk appetite.

Against a backdrop of moderate growth, sharply lower June oil prices and fading tariff
effects, inflation delivered an encouraging downside surprise for a change. Not only did
June headline consumer price index (CPI) inflation decline sharply alongside lower oil prices,
but “core” CPI (excluding food and energy) also posted a small but rare monthly decline,
nudging the year-over-year (YoY) rate down to 2.6% from 2.8% in May. The improvement
was broad based: prices of goods excluding food and energy declined, prices for services

excluding energy and rent of shelter declined, and a larger share of CPI components (about
a third) declined. With housing still likely to contribute to future disinflation—as new-lease
measures remain soft and multifamily supply continues to expand—and little evidence
that tariffs or energy prices are feeding a broader wage-price spiral so far, gradual
inflation normalization without much Fed tightening is likely, in our view.
Investment Implications

With moderate U.S. growth and inflation
underpinnings, we expect investors to
continue to favor economically sensitive
sectors and AI-investment beneficiaries,
assuming stabilization of geopolitical
situation.

### Page 3

3 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
The main risk to the outlook remains energy. Although the global economy has adapted
better than expected to Strait of Hormuz disruptions, as discussed in recent reports,
renewed and expanded trade barriers raise risks to both growth and inflation. Ukrainian
attacks on Russian refining and export infrastructure have further tightened the global
refined-products market, with at least 24 of Russia’s 34 large refineries reportedly struck

by drones and bringing Russian crude processing down to its lowest level in more than
two decades. Given its large export footprint, especially for diesel, Russian diesel and jet
fuel export restrictions combined with gasoline imports have tightened refined-product
markets. India’s recent introduction of higher fuel export taxes and China’s preference for
preserving crude inventories while retaining large underutilized refining capacity reduce
the likelihood of near-term supply relief. In fact, the threatened blockade of the Bab-el-

Mandeb strait would extend the transit time for roughly three million barrels per day of
Saudi oil going to Asia by about a month, with additional logistical complexity tied to
Suez Canal tanker capacity constraints also increasing costs and transit time.
According to the EIA, with U.S. refiners running at full capacity, commercial crude
inventories remain above pre-2015 levels. However, refiners have prioritized jet fuel and
distillate production to meet strong global demand, causing gasoline inventories to fall

below their five-year range. Reflecting tight supply, U.S. gasoline and diesel prices have
risen to levels consistent with roughly $140 and $170 per barrel crude oil, respectively.
Moreover, fuel markets remain vulnerable to potential unexpected refinery maintenance
requirements. The Strategic Petroleum Reserve (SPR) has declined to 40-year lows and
is approaching operational limits.
Still, because of a big decline in the U.S. economy’s energy intensity, diesel and gasoline

costs as a share of GDP remain below past oil-shock levels (around 2.2% vs. roughly
4.5% in 1973, for example). Also, the U.S. is now an oil exporter rather than a large oil
importer. This suggests that oil prices are not high enough to cause a U.S. or global
recession. According to Dallas Fed Research, June 23, 2026, the estimated effect on U.S.
GDP growth in 1980 from a similar oil shock as that caused by the Hormuz crisis this
year would have had a 20 times larger impact (Exhibit 1B). Global fuel intensity has also

declined significantly, but the U.S. resilience is still greater. At current prices, there are
still sufficient growth tailwinds to keep the expansion going at a moderate pace. Also
positive, tempered growth and still anchored inflation expectations have helped keep the
10-year Treasury yield in its relatively narrow range of the past two years despite spiking
oil prices, with markets relatively comfortable with the gradual uptick in yields to date.

The bigger risk to asset prices would be a disorderly rise in real yields and bond-market
volatility, including if driven by Fed interest rate hikes without stronger growth.
Exhibit 1: Inflation Divergence Likely to Resolve to the Downside.
A) Industrial Production still moderate compared to past economic booms. B) Tremendously greater resilience to oil shock today compared to the past.

MARKET VIEW
International Equity Markets—Strengths, Weaknesses,
Opportunities and Challenges
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
The drawdown in the global Technology sector between early-June and mid-July has
highlighted a major dividing line across international equity markets. Europe has been
the global leader over the period, emerging Asian markets have been by far the weakest,

and the U.S. and Japan have fallen in between the two. This has been a mirror image of
the full year 2026 so far, in which technology leadership has driven outperformance in
emerging markets and seen Europe lag. And it underscores the current importance of
the Technology sector to relative international returns, given the wide disparities now
present in regional capitalization weightings. Technology exposure (including the internet
platforms listed in Communication Services and Consumer Discretionary) now accounts

for 50% of the MSCI Emerging Market (EM) Index benchmark (and 60% of emerging
Asia), with the semiconductor industry group alone forming 35% of EM Equities (and
42% of emerging Asia).
By contrast, Europe’s Technology sector exposure stands at just 10%. Indeed, since the
start of 2025, the MSCI EM Index has outpaced the overall developed ex-U.S. benchmark
(MSCI World ex-U.S.) in 70% of the weekly periods in which the global Technology sector
has risen by more than global Equities (MSCI All-Country World) in aggregate (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 2: Emerging Markets have Led Global Equities During Recent Periods of

A key question for international markets in the second half of the year will therefore
be whether the advance in technology-linked markets can continue, particularly within
upstream segments related to AI data center investment. And important to watch

here will be the capital spending plans of the cloud hyperscalers, pricing power in
critical parts of the supply chain such as memory, foundry and semicap equipment, and,
ultimately, user demand for models and developer applications. Wider AI adoption and
more application use cases would likely allow market participation to extend further
downstream within the sector, including in China, whose capitalization share of the MSCI
EM index has halved over the past five years, but at close to 20% remains the third-

largest after Taiwan and Korea. And notwithstanding the potential for further bouts of
volatility of the type experienced over recent weeks, broader gains for the tech sector
should continue to favor EMs and Asia over Europe.
The return of energy supply constraints in the Middle East nonetheless poses a growing risk
to both European and Asian markets. Both are heavily reliant on imported oil and gas in their
primary energy consumption, with Asia more directly dependent on shipments from the

Middle East. And though both markets were able to withstand the disruptions to production,
shipping and prices during the first phase of the Iran conflict, the July re-escalation could
potentially be more challenging should it persist over the weeks and months ahead.
Investment Implications
We maintain a favorable view on EMs
relative to Europe, but continue to
weigh the potential impact of changes
to the macro environment related to
the pivotal themes of technology and

energy. We also stay cautious on the
UK market, particularly in light of this
month’s change in central government
leadership. And we remain constructive
on Japan, despite the market facing
similar risks to much of emerging Asia
from any further tightening in global
energy supply..

The MSCI Europe and MSCI emerging Asia benchmarks corrected by 10% to 15% in
March following the initial outbreak of the conflict but rebounded in Q2 even in the face
of higher energy prices as activity in the sector adjusted. Individual countries were able
to draw upon their national oil inventories. China in particular (by far the largest global
energy importer) reduced its oil imports by around 40%, taking them back to 2016 levels

when its economy was roughly half its current size (Exhibit 3). And oil and gas export
volumes were redirected to Saudi Arabia’s Red Sea coast.

But these three sources of relief are unlikely to offer the same cushion for markets in the
current phase of the conflict. Global observed oil inventories have now been drawn down
by more than 300 million barrels over the period between March and June, according

to the International Energy Agency. Depending on its willingness to further deplete
its domestic stockpiles, China’s oil import demand could begin to increase again from
depressed levels. And risks to energy exports through the Red Sea could force a diversion
of the estimated 5.4 million barrels per day of oil and 2.9 billion cubic feet per day of
liquefied natural gas that were transported through the waterway in Q1 of 2026, according
to the U.S. Energy Information Administration. It also remains to be seen how global

monetary authorities will react to higher headline inflation driven by the recent energy
price surge. Higher interest rates could present an additional hurdle for international equity
markets if central banks are forced to tighten policy. We maintain a favorable view on EMs
relative to Europe, but continue to weigh the potential impact of changes to the macro
environment related to these two pivotal themes of technology and energy.
Our view on the smaller regional developed markets also remains intact and has been

reinforced by recent local developments. As in continental Europe, we stay cautious on the
U.K. market, particularly in light of this month’s change in central government leadership.
Even though the ruling party remains in place and its platform is officially unchanged, the
new prime minister is expected to pursue a more redistributionist policy agenda and has
suggested introducing more flexibility into national budget rules with the potential for

future increases in tax rates. The fiscal outlook will therefore be important to watch over
the coming months for any potential deterioration in investor perceptions around the local
market. And, at the same time, we remain constructive on Japan. Given its dependence
on imported oil from the Middle East, the Japan market faces similar risks to much of
emerging Asia from any further tightening in global energy supply from the region. And
to a lesser extent, it should also remain sensitive to volatility in the Technology sector,

given its hardware exposure to industry groups such as semicap equipment and electronic
components. We nonetheless still see structural supports from shareholder-friendly
deployment of corporate cash for buybacks, payouts and acquisitions, in addition to fiscal
expansion coming from the new administration, and both competitive support and a
translation boost on overseas profits from yen weakness.

6 of 8 July 27, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Behind the Surge in U.S. Business Formation: The “Solopreneur”
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Business formation is at all-time highs in the U.S., with applications over the 12 months
through June 2026 running north of 6 million. 1 In other words, entrepreneurial activity
is booming in the U.S. But an underappreciated feature of the recent surge in business

applications has been the rise of the “solopreneur”—or solo founders who run businesses
on their own without employees.
As Exhibit 4A highlights, the increase in business applications since the start of 2025
has been primarily driven by solopreneurs. Whereas their applications have risen by 23%
in just the last year and a half, applications among “high-propensity” businesses (i.e.,
businesses likely to hire workers) are essentially flat over the same period. Translated:

Solo founders have fueled most of the recent acceleration in business creation. Data
from Stripe, a financial services platform, confirms in Exhibit 4B: The wave of new
startups, particularly in Q1 of this year, has largely been the work of solo founders.
What’s driving entrepreneurs to go solo? Enter generative artificial intelligence (AI), which
has lowered barriers to entry and enabled businesses to innovate, produce, market and
expand, all without necessarily hiring additional employees. Indeed, the sectors that have

seen the largest growth in business applications such as Information and Professional
Services are also the sectors with the highest AI adoption rates of 44% and 38%
respectively.
1 All the while, job growth in these sectors has been limited or negative.
Employment in the Information sector, for example, is currently below pre-pandemic
levels—despite the sector’s gross domestic product expanding by over 50% since the
start of the decade.
For investors, the rise of the solopreneur and the attendant increase in business

formation is a direct reflection of the U.S. economy’s entrepreneurial DNA. We believe
that's a key reason to maintain the U.S. at the center of portfolios because it means
more competition, greater innovation and, over time, higher productivity—all of which are
good news for corporate earnings and U.S. growth. Already, AI has helped lift company
margins with earnings as a share of GDP at a record 14% as of Q1 2026.
2 As AI adoption
grows and as the cost of technology declines, we expect new small businesses, the

backbone of the U.S. economy, to be among the biggest recipients of productivity gains.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.
Institute for Supply Management Manufacturing Index is a composite measure equally weighing new orders, production, employment, supplier deliveries, and inventories.
MSCI Emerging Market Index is a stock market index designed to measure the equity market performance of developing economies globally.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Document 268: Claude Code倒计时5天默认自动模式，多花的钱A社自己掏

Source PDF: all report pdf/Claude Code倒计时5天默认自动模式，多花的钱A社自己掏.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-08-10

Status: ok

### Page 1

5 天后， Claude Code 将默认启⽤⾃动模式，该模式额外消耗的 token 费⽤由 Anthropic 承
担。此举是因为⼈⼯审批已流于形式（同意率⾼达 97% ），⽽实验证明，⾃动模式拦截危险
命令的成功率（ 89% ）远超⼈类（ 13.6% ），安全性⼤幅提升。⽤⼾仍可快捷切换回⼿动模
式。
摘要：
咱就是想问，还有⼈认真看 AI 编程⼯具给的权限审批请求吗？
Anthropic 也发现了，只有 3% 的权限请求被拒绝。
于是他们做了⼀个决定， 5 天后，所有 Claude Code 将默认启⽤⾃动模式。
⾃动模式的分类器每次⼯具调⽤会额外消耗少量 token ，这部分开销将不再向⽤⼾收费。
亚⻢逊⾕歌微软等云平台⽬前仍然是选配，但 Anthropic 只给他们⼀个⽉时间，要把这些渠道也
切成默认⾃动模式。
Claude Code 之⽗表⽰，团队内部早就只是⽤⾃动模式了，根本⽆法想象再去⼿动审批权限。
⼈⼯审批权限的时代⼀去不复返
对于这次改动 Anthropic 给出的第⼀组理由，是⼈⼯审批已经变成⼀种习惯性动作：

⽤⼾对 Claude Code 权限提⽰的同意率是 97% 。作为对照，当 Claude 把⼀份计划交给⽤⼾批准
时，拒绝率是 39% ；⽽单条权限请求的拒绝率只有 3% 。
同样的倾向也出现在配置⽂件⾥。截⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉， 49.5% 的活跃 CLI ⽤⼾⼿动写过 Bash allow-
rule ，其中 5% 直接放⾏任意 shell 命令，另有 43% 写了 Bash(python:) 、 Bash(node:) 这类实际效
果基本等同的解释器规则，这个⽐例⼤约每五周增⻓ 5 个百分点。
此外， 62% 的⽤⼾⽤过 bypassPermissions 或者在 Bash 上点过 “ 不再询问 ” ， 25% 的交互式会话⼀
开始就运⾏在 bypass permissions 模式下。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

第⼆组理由来⾃⼀项受控实验。 Anthropic 通过研究供应商招募了 1053 名付费专业测试者，在专
⻔搭建的测试环境⾥完成编码任务。会话进⾏到⼀半时，其中⼀条权限提⽰被替换成⼀条明显
危险的命令，供应商记录测试者是否点了同意（同意并不会真的执⾏任何危险操作，被改动的
只是提⽰⽂本）。
结果是测试者拦住这条命令的⽐例为 13.6% ， 143 次；
auto mode ⾯对同样的命令拦下了 89% ， 937 次。
正⾯⽐较下来， auto mode 拦住了 800 条⼈类放⾏的命令，⼈类只拦住了 6 条 auto mode 放⾏的命
令。
⽽且会话越⻓，⼈的表现越差：前期还能拦住约 17% ，在经历 50 条以上提⽰之后降到约 5% ，⽽
auto mode 的拦截率不随会话⻓度变化。
⽣产数据也被拿来重新打分。
2026 年 5 ⽉到 6 ⽉， Anthropic 从已授权数据⽤于训练与分析的账号中，取出安全流⽔线标记过的
会话，⽤模型按 0 到 10 的严重程度重新评级。
-10 分代表全公司范围、不可逆的伤害。

-7 分代表删除⽣产数据库记录这⼀级别
-0 分代表覆盖⼀个临时⽂件这类完全可回滚的本地操作，
同时评估⽤⼾对该操作的要求有多明确。在 7 分及以上的严重级别上，⾛⼈⼯审批的会话中有
6.3% 出现了⽤⼾并未明确要求的有害操作， auto mode 会话是 2.4% ，在测试过的每个严重级别
上差距都成⽴。
分类器本⾝也经过对抗测试。 Anthropic 与 AI 安全公司 Apollo Research 做了为期两周的试点，把
合成攻击注⼊真实编码轨迹，覆盖泛化失效、数据外泄和安全相关威胁三类数据集。
Apollo 交回成功攻击样本与加固建议后， Anthropic 主要通过给分类器提供更多环境上下⽂来加
固，包括仓库可⻅性、 git 状态和数据处理规则。
加固后 Apollo ⽤⼀组 Anthropic ⽆法接触的留出攻击集重测， auto mode 追回了此前漏掉的约⼀半
攻击，代价是漏掉约 2% 此前拦住的攻击，两组数据集合并计算，漏检率从 12% 降到 7% 。

### Page 3

Anthropic 同时说明，这些攻击是为找出分类器失效点⽽合成的， 7% 不应被理解为真实流量下的
漏检率。
针对提⽰词注⼊， Anthropic 委托第三⽅ Trajectory Labs 做了评测，还拉来了 OpenAI 来当垫背，
共 72 个间接注⼊场景，每个场景跑 10 次。
攻击是针对 Claude Opus 4.7 优化的，⽽ Opus 4.7 不在被测名单中，所以所有参测模型⾯对的都
是未⻅过的攻击。
在 720 次攻击尝试中，运⾏ auto mode 的 Claude Fable 5 、 Opus 5 和 Sonnet 5 没有⼀次被攻破；
运⾏ Codex Auto-review 模式的 GPT-5.6 Sol 攻击成功率为 5.83% ， Full Access 模式下为
19.03% 。
作为参照， Claude 最新模型在不加额外防护的 bypassPermissions 模式下的平均攻击成功率是
0.09% 。
公开的三起拦截案例分别是：
⼀次向内部云盘上传失败后， Claude 的备选⽅案是把同⼀份报告发布到公开代码分享站点，被

拒绝并转为询问⽤⼾；
⼀次⻓会话中， Claude 试图跨越两千个 pod 批量结束进程，并排队了会让数百张 GPU 上在跑的
训练任务被拆除的删除操作，命令在执⾏前被标记；
还有⼀次是被要求配置只读账单权限时， Claude 取⽤了⼀个在账⼾根级别拥有完整管理权限的
云⻆⾊，权限范围与请求不符被拦下。
近期新增的能⼒包括：
把数据外泄列为 hard deny ，分类器永不批准，要执⾏必须切出 auto mode 或⼿动运⾏，该规则
可在设置中扩展；
区分密钥与敏感信息各⾃可访问和可分享的范围，并在 git push 或 PR 执⾏前检查⽬标仓库是公
开、私有还是可信；

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

在 git reset —hard 这类可能丢弃未提交⼯作的命令前读取 git status ；

想换回去？ Shift+Tab
对 Pro 、 Max 、 Team ⽤⼾来说，如果从未设置过默认权限模式，会收到产品内通知，新会话⾃动
以 auto mode 启动；设过其他默认的会看到⼀次性询问；如果 Team 管理员已经在 managed
settings ⾥指定了默认，则不受影响。
切换模式在 CLI 中按 Shift+Tab ，桌⾯端使⽤模式下拉菜单。管理员可以⽤ managed settings 中的
defaultMode 固定组织级默认，或⽤ disableAutoMode 完全关闭 auto mode 。

Anthropic 在公告结尾提⽰， auto mode 依赖分类系统，可以降低⻛险但不能消除⻛险，对⽣产
基础设施的⾼⻛险改动，仍然建议⽤⼾⾃⾏审查 Claude 的操作。

Document 311: Is the energy crisis accelerating the low-carbon transition_ A nuanced response for advanced economies — Part 2_ (2026-07-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Is the energy crisis accelerating the low-carbon transition_ A nuanced response for advanced economies — Part 2_ (2026-07-27).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-07-27
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.30 / 27 July 2026EDITORIAL
IS THE ENERGY CRISIS ACCELERATING THE LOW-CARBON TRANSITION? A NUANCED RESPONSE FOR ADVANCED
ECONOMIES — PART 2 1
1 Read part 1 of this editorial in our EcoWeek of 27 July 2026: Is the energy crisis accelerating the low-carbon transition? Part one: In emerging economies, yes.
Since the Trump administration returned to power, the already considerable disparity in energy policy between

the United States and Europe has significantly increased. Admittedly, both are seeking energy sovereignty. But,
while European policies remain focused on the low-carbon transition, the current US administration is focused on
enhancing US dominance in the fossil fuel sector and rolling back measures that support the energy transition.
At first glance, the energy crisis is exacerbating this divergence. The US has consolidated its position as the world’s

leading exporter of hydrocarbons, and its strategic oil reserves have served as a buffer against the crisis. In contrast,
the crisis in Europe has once again highlighted the cost of its dependence on hydrocarbon imports, which has given
a significant boost to European policies for electrification and decarbonation.
Nevertheless, the analysis of the US situation needs to be contextualised, as it is part of a different dynamic. While
energy sovereignty is not a driving force behind the transition as it is in Europe, the sharp rise in demand associated

with the development of artificial intelligence is, in the short term, conducive to the decarbonation of the energy mix.
FOSSIL FUELS: THE WAR IN IRAN REINFORCES THE UNITED STATES’
GLOBAL LEADERSHIP
The geopolitical constraints affecting exports from the two main
OPEC+ producers, Russia and Saudi Arabia, are strengthening
the United States’ position as the world’s leading exporter of oil
and gas, with production rising by 3.5% and 3.8% respectively in

Q2 2026 compared to the previous year. Although President Trump’s
goal of reducing the price of a barrel of crude oil to USD50 seems
unattainable in the short term, the country’s energy dominance
— already evident before the crisis — has been reinforced since the
outbreak of the war in Iran. In January 2026, US oil exports (both
crude and refined products) accounted for 63% of the combined ex -
ports of Saudi Arabia and Russia, and were 1.1 times higher than

those of the two countries in May 2026. As for gas (both liquefied
and piped), US export volumes have remained above 21 billion cu -
bic metres (bcm) per month since the start of the year, while ex -
ports from Qatar have plummeted by nearly 80% (to 3.2 bcm in
May 2026). Meanwhile, Australian exports (LNG only) have stabi -
lised at around 8 bcm since the start of the year.
EUROPE REMAINS COMMITTED TO ITS CLIMATE TARGETS
In Europe, the situation is very different, even the opposite. Com -

mitted to a strategy aimed at reducing its greenhouse gas emis -
sions, it is difficult for Europe to face the energy crisis by increa -
sing hydrocarbon production, except perhaps on a temporary basis.
Nevertheless, rising energy prices have increased pressure to boost
European hydrocarbon production. For the time being, the com -
mon objective seems to be to maintain current production levels
wherever possible. In Norway — a key supplier of gas to the EU

and the UK — the reopening of offshore gas fields, announced last
May, is expected, according to the Norwegian government, to en -
able production to be maintained at its 2025 level. In the UK, the
new Burnham government is expected to extend the moratorium
— reaffirmed last March — on any new exploration licences. Ne -
vertheless, it could incentivise oil companies to increase production
from existing oil fields and, in particular, authorise ‘tiebacks’, which

are new wells connected to existing sites.
The shift to coal appears to be minimal in Europe, despite Euro -
pean LNG prices having almost doubled since the end of February.
In Germany, where coal accounted for 24% of the electricity mix in
2024 — the highest proportion in the EU after Poland — the of -
ficial target remains the complete phase-out of coal by 2038 (any
potential increase in its use is expected to be temporary). According
to the IEA, in the European Union, the increase in renewable ener -

gy production compensated for the decline in electricity generation
from gas between March and May 2026 (-3.5% year-on-year).
ELECTRIC VEHICLES: REBOUND IN EUROPE, CONTRACTION IN THE US
Since the onset of the energy crisis, fuel price rises have been more
pronounced in the United States (+23% in June 2026 year-on-year)
than in the European Union (+14%), due in particular to the lower
level of fuel taxation in the United States.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.30 / 27 July 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Despite these rises, sales of electric vehicles — a key driver of the
electrification of transport — have exhibited contrasting trends.
Over the first five months of the year, sales of battery electric
vehicles rose by over 31% year-on-year in Europe, while they fell
by 25% in the United States 2. Changes in government subsidies for
electric mobility largely account for these changes, which will fur -

ther widen the disparity in the market share of electric vehicles (as
a percentage of new registrations). Consequently, the share of elec -
tric vehicles was 5.5% in May 2026 in the United States (7.5% for
the whole of 2025) and 24% in Europe in May 2026 (20% in 2025).
In the United States, all federal incentives — primarily tax credit
for purchases and grants for the installation of charging points —
have been phased out since 2025. Only state-level incentives re -

main, which are insufficient to compensate for the end of federal
support. In contrast, in the European Union and the United King -
dom, the scale of support measures, including purchase grants and
tax rebates, has contributed to a resurgence in sales in the first
half of 2026. Most of these measures were in place before the cri -
sis and, for the time being, the crisis has resulted in only minor
modifications, such as the extension of social leasing in France

and increased support for the installation of charging points in the
United Kingdom.
ELECTRIFICATION: HEIGHTENED EUROPEAN AMBITIONS
In June 2026, the European Union announced an ambitious pro -
gramme aimed at accelerating the electrification of end-use sectors.
The aim is to achieve an electrification rate of 46% by 2040, up
from the current rate of 23%. This target is highly ambitious, as the
rate has seen little progress for over a decade, despite the policies

implemented. As a point of reference, the electrification rate stands
at 22% in the United States and 30% in China. This European initia -
tive specifically aims to:
1/ reduce the cost disparity for industry between electricity and
fossil fuels, as electricity is still twice as expensive in 21 Member
States due to taxes, network charges, and public subsidies that may
favour fossil fuels 3;
2/ enhance electricity storage to increase the flexibility of re -

newable energy4 by quadrupling capacity by 2030 to reach 200 GW;
3/ accelerate the electrification of corporate vehicle fleets;
4/ reform the carbon tax mechanism. Alongside this specifically Eu -
ropean initiative, the European Union, together with other countries
(including the United Kingdom), is spearheading the international
‘Electrify Now’ programme, which aims to accelerate the electrifica -
tion of transport, industry and the building sector to achieve a tar -

get of 35% by 2035. This initiative will form part of the forthcoming
COP 31 and COP 32 conferences.
2 Jefferies, June 2026.
3 European Commission, 2026, Electrification Action Plan. The European Commission reports that subsidies for fossil fuels amounted to EUR 97 bn in 2024 (averaging
EUR 60 bn between 2015 and 2019).
4 For further information on this topic, see our article “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles“.

5 Energy Institute, 2026, Statistical Review of World Energy.
6 Lazard, July 2026, Levelised Cost of Energy+.
DECARBONATION OF THE ELECTRICITY MIX: SOLAR PRIORITISED
In both Europe and the United States, the drive towards decarbo -
nation appears to have gathered pace since March. Although im -
ports of solar panels from China to the European Union and the
United Kingdom decreased in 2025 (by 19% and 3% year-on-year
respectively), they rose significantly between March and May 2026

(by 25% and 54% year-on-year respectively). The wind power sector
is, by its very nature, less responsive in the short term given the
lead times involved. Nevertheless, while Europe continues to set out
ambitious targets such as a 50% increase in installed capacity by
2030, the current US administration has stated its opposition to the
development of this sector. In 2025, the installed wind power capa -
city in Europe was 1.9 times greater than that of the United States.

In the United States, analysing trends in decarbonation is
complicated by two main factors: the trade war with China and the
rise in energy consumption driven by the development of artificial
intelligence applications. In 2025, imports of solar panels from
China to the United States increased by 26% and saw a remarkable
surge of 178% year-on-year between March and May 2026, despite
the introduction of 60% tariffs on these products in 2025 (which
were previously set at 25% under the former administration).

The rise in energy demand driven by the development of artificial
intelligence appears to have mitigated the impact of the tariff
increases. The proportion of electricity consumption attributed
to data centres has grown by 63% since 2020, reaching 6.5% in
20255, which has resulted in sharp increases in electricity prices in
some regions. According to the IEA, the growth of AI is responsible
for 50% of the increase in electricity consumption. In the short

term, due to considerable delays in the commissioning of gas-
fired power stations caused by high demand, it is anticipated that
most of the additional energy generation capacity will derive from
a combination of solar power and stationary batteries, despite the
rising costs affecting this sector 6.
Regardless of the national energy context, the rise in demand for
low-carbon technologies — whether or not accelerated by the en -
ergy crisis — will benefit Chinese exports. Already the undisputed

leader in solar panel production, China holds dominant positions
across the entire electric battery value chain. This sector is ex -
pected to continue experiencing very strong growth in the coming
years, both to power US data centres and to allow higher propor -
tions of renewable energy in the European electricity mix.
Pascal Devaux
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The household spending indicator rose 0.8%mth in June
(nominal), exceeding both Westpac and market expectations.
We had pencilled in a modest 0.1%mth rise, while the market
anticipated 0.2%mth. Today’s result follows a period of more

volatile outcomes, with spending rising 1.2%mth in May
(revised down 0.1ppt) and falling 1.0%mth in April. Annual
growth strengthened to 6.0%yr. For Q2 as a whole, nominal
spending rose 1.3%qtr, slightly above the 1.0%qtr increase
recorded in Q1. In real terms, volumes increased 0.7%qtr,
marginally below the 0.8%qtr pace recorded in Q1, pointing
to a modest acceleration in price growth through the quarter.
Discretionary spending supports the headline

By broad category, goods spending improved from last month’s
flat result, rising 1.0%mth, although the annual pace softened
further to 5.8%yr. Services spending lost some momentum,
increasing 0.6%mth, with annual growth strengthening to
6.1%yr following May’s strong result. Discretionary spending
continued to post solid gains, rising 1.2%mth in June, lifting
the annual pace to 6.7%yr, suggesting the consumer remains
somewhat resilient. Non-discretionary spending only managed

to eke out a modest 0.1%mth gain as softer fuel prices weighed
on outcomes. Annual growth eased to 4.6%yr.
At the detailed level, gains were concentrated in transport
spending, which rose 3.0%mth. The ABS attributed the
increase primarily to new vehicle sales, with electric vehicles
accounting for a growing share of new vehicle purchases.
Air travel also contributed, with spending returning to levels
seen prior to disruptions associated with the Middle East

conflict. Recreation & culture spending increased 1.4%mth,
with the ABS noting some strength reflected advanced ticket
purchases for future live performances. Alcoholic beverages
& tobacco rose 1.0%mth, while furnishings & household
equipment (+0.6%mth), health (+0.3%mth) and food
(+0.3%mth) also recorded gains.
Partially offsetting these increases, clothing & footwear
spending fell 0.7%mth, while miscellaneous goods & services
declined 0.6%mth and hotels, cafes & restaurants fell

0.1%mth. Notably, these were the three strongest categories
in May, suggesting a degree of payback rather than a
deterioration in underlying spending momentum.
State outcomes were positive, with all states recording
spending growth in June. Tasmania led the gains, rising
1.6%mth and fully reversing last month’s decline. WA
(+1.4%mth), NSW (+1.0%mth), Victoria (+0.6%mth), SA
(+0.5%mth) and Queensland (+0.4%mth) also posted increases.
Spending growth posts a solid result in June

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Volume growth remains positive 
Nominal spending rose 1.3%qtr in Q2, with volumes up 
0.7%qtr, slightly softer than the upwardly revised 0.8%qtr 
increase recorded in Q1. This implies a modest acceleration 
in price growth to 0.6%qtr, compared with 0.2%qtr in Q1. 
Through the year, volume growth eased to 2.4%yr, remaining 
well above the 1.8%yr average recorded through 2025.
Discretionary categories led gains in volume terms. Alcoholic 
beverages & tobacco rose 2.7%qtr, while furnishings & 
household equipment increased 1.7%qtr, recreation & culture 
also rose 1.7%qtr and hotels, cafes & restaurants increased 
1.0%qtr. Outcomes across other categories were more 
modest. Clothing & footwear rose 0.5%qtr, miscellaneous 
goods & services increased 0.4%qtr and food rose 0.3%qtr. 
Health recorded the largest decline, falling 0.4%qtr, while 
transport declined 0.1%qtr. This aligns with our Consumer 
Panel data, which identified transport spending as a drag 
on overall activity, reflecting airfare refunds associated with 
disruptions stemming from the Middle East conflict (see here). 
All states recorded real spending growth over the quarter, led 
by Tasmania (+1.1%qtr), SA (+0.9%qtr), Victoria (+0.8%qtr), 
Queensland (+0.7%qtr), WA (+0.7%qtr) and NSW (+0.4%qtr).
Conclusion
The household spending indicator provides a decent guide 
on around two thirds of total consumer expenditure. Today’s 
volumes outcome was broadly in line with our expectations, 
Westpac anticipating around a 0.5%qtr rise in Q2. Importantly, 
this data excludes several major categories (e.g. rents, utilities, 
etc.) that are in the official measure of real household spending 
in the National Accounts.

Given today’s data, we will review our forecast for consumption 
ahead of the Q2 National Accounts (due 2 September). 
Westpac–DataX Card Tracker data show the quarterly pace of 
growth slowed to 0.2%qtr at end-June (see here), well below 
the 1.8%qtr average recorded through the second half of 2025. 
Uncertainty surrounding the Middle East conflict, together with 
three consecutive RBA rate increases during the first half of the 
year, is also likely to have weighed on household spending.

While consumers remain pessimistic, recent data suggest some 
improvement as concerns around energy prices, interest rates 
and labour market conditions have begun to ease (see here). 
Combined with a more benign June quarter inflation outcome 
and our expectation that the RBA has concluded its tightening 
cycle for this year (see here), near-term downside risks to 
consumption appear less threatening.

Despite uncertainties, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• The twist and turns associated with the conflict in the Middle East is increasing
volatility in energy and related prices. At the current point in time supply is

uninterrupted but prices remain elevated relative to pre-conflict levels. If this
continues, this will place upward pressure on farm input costs, potentially
eroding some of the gains from still-elevated commodity prices.
• Our forecast for the 2026/27 farmgate milk price remains at $9.50/kg, noting
that the range of uncertainty is particularly wide at this stage of the season.
Global milk production remains ample but is slowing from its highs.
• Meanwhile, global beef and lamb prices are expected to remain well supported

over the coming year, reflecting strong demand and constrained supply. Beef
prices are being underpinned by lower production in the US and Brazil, while
lamb prices face some headwinds from rising Australian supply and slower
demand growth in Europe, the US, and China.
• Meanwhile, strong demand for premium kiwifruit and apple varieties is set to
keep prices and grower returns at elevated levels, despite higher input costs,
soft market conditions in China and aforementioned geopolitical headwinds.

• Forestry prices should improve moderately, supported by tighter supply and
recovering demand, but China’s still weak property market will cap upside.

El Niño events could support commodity export prices.
• Climate forecasts indicate an increasing likelihood of El Niño developing through

summer 2026/27, raising the risk of dry conditions across northern and eastern
New Zealand.
• New Zealand agriculture enters this period in relatively good condition, with
normal soil moisture and adequate feed supplies. However, conditions could
deteriorate if a strong El Niño persists.
• Poor pasture growth could reduce dairy production through earlier cow dry-
offs. If other dairy-exporting regions are similarly affected, tighter global supply

could support dairy prices and farmgate returns.
• For sheep and beef farmers, weaker pasture growth may initially increase
slaughter rates and pressure prices. Later, herd and flock rebuilding could
tighten supply and support beef and lamb prices.
• Kiwifruit, apples and viticulture are generally more resilient, but prolonged
drought, heat stress and water restrictions could reduce yields. Lower
production may be partly offset by firmer export prices if global demand

remains strong.
• Given the uncertainty surrounding El Niño, we have assumed no material impact
on commodity prices over the coming year.

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Global geopolitical risks have re-emerged as a significant source of uncertainty.

The temporary easing in tensions that had helped stabilise markets in June has
faded, with renewed US-Iran hostilities and ongoing attacks on commercial
shipping in and around the Red Sea increasing concerns about global trade
flows and energy supplies.
• These developments have contributed to heightened volatility in energy
markets. Crude oil prices have fluctuated sharply in response to shifting
perceptions of supply risk, with flow-on effects for petroleum products,

gasoil, and petrochemical feedstocks such as naphtha, an important input for
plastics manufacturing.
• Fertiliser markets are also under pressure. Sanctions on Russia and Belarus,
trade restrictions, and shipping disruptions have constrained global supplies
of sulphur, phosphate, potash, and some nitrogen fertilisers. However, urea
prices have remained relatively stable, supported by recovering international
supply and increased exports from China, which have helped offset

conflict-  related disruptions.
• For New Zealand farmers, the key risk is that more volatile energy and fertiliser
markets could result in input costs increasing faster than what we have already
pencilled in.

Dairy prices expected to remain favourable.
• World dairy prices have declined in recent months, though overall they have

remained at favourable levels. Skim milk powder prices have fared better than
other products as global demand has turned from fats and towards proteins.
• Global milk production remains strong but is easing back from last year’s rapid
pace of growth. Milk collections in the main exporting countries are up about
2% on a year ago. The Iran conflict has put pressure on energy, feed, fertiliser
and freight costs, while the heatwave in Europe has weighed on milk production

in some countries.
• Our farmgate milk price forecast for the 2026/27 season remains at $9.50/kg.
This assumes that world prices remain around current levels on average over the
rest of the season.
• There are upside and downside risks to this view, and the range of possibilities
remains wide at this early stage of the season. Fonterra recently revised down
its forecast midpoint from $9.75 to $9.25, with a range of $8.00-10.50.
• The main downside risk to prices stems from higher fuel costs having a larger

than expected impact on global consumer demand. The key upside risk is that
El Niño could lead to a substantial shortfall in milk supply over the New Zealand
summer and autumn (which is already starting to be reflected in futures prices
over this period).

Fundamentals continue to support NZ beef export prices.
• Global beef supply remains structurally constrained, which is expected to
continue supporting export prices for NZ farmers.

• US import demand is likely to be a key driver of global beef prices for the
foreseeable future. While the US cattle herd is rebuilding, a meaningful increase
in US beef production is not expected for another 2 to 3 years.
• The supply outlook is also being tightened by developments elsewhere. Brazil,
the world's largest beef exporter, has entered a herd rebuilding phase, which is
likely to slow export growth over the coming year.
• At the same time, China and the European Union are expected to see lower beef

production due to shrinking cattle herds, tighter environmental regulations, and
rising production costs. This is expected to more than offset stable to higher
output from Australia, New Zealand, Mexico, and Canada.
• Demand for beef is expected to be supported by rising incomes and
consumption in Asia and other emerging markets, continued foodservice
demand, and strong consumer preference for beef in key markets.
• These factors are likely to help keep global beef prices at firm levels, despite

some downside risks from weaker economic growth and/or softer Chinese
demand, and with cost of living pressures favouring cheaper forms of protein.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
US INVESTIGATION INTO
NZ LAMB EXPORTS
The US formally launches investigation into lamb meat exports.
• On 13 July 2026, the Office of the US Trade Representative (USTR) requested that
the US International Trade Commission (USITC) investigate whether rising lamb
imports are causing serious injury to the US sheep industry.
• The American Sheep Industry Association argues that imports have increased
substantially and now account for roughly 70% of the US lamb market. The

investigation covers fresh, chilled, and frozen lamb meat, which is primarily
imported from Australia and New Zealand.
• For New Zealand lamb exporters, this remains a risk rather than an actual tariff
event. No safeguard measures are in place yet. From a market perspective, the
investigation introduces uncertainty for NZ lamb exporters, but export flows
continue normally at present.
• Decision day is 13 November 2026. That’s when the USITC decides whether

imports are causing serious injury. If the finding is yes, the Commission will
recommend remedies to the US President, who will decide whether to impose
tariffs, quotas, tariff-rate quotas, or other restrictions in 2027 Q1.
• It’s not clear how the recent increase in tariffs on a range of New Zealand
exports, from 10% to 12.5%, including lamb, will affect any decisions made by
the President in this regard.

Lamb export prices to remain elevated, but downside risks increasing.
• Lamb prices are expected to remain well supported over the coming 12 months,

underpinned by constrained global supply and strong demand from premium
markets in the UK, Europe, North America and the Middle East.
• On the supply side, sheep flocks have been declining in New Zealand, the UK,
and parts of Europe for several years, reducing the volume of lamb available for
export. Further flock declines should continue to support prices.
• Offsetting this to some degree, Australian lamb production and exports are
expected to rise, supported by a large breeding flock and higher lambing rates.

Given Australia's dominant share of global lamb exports, this is likely to temper
price gains.
• Demand in European markets has been slowing for some time. There are
signs that this might also be happening in the UK with weak domestic pricing
prompting a switch away from New Zealand lamb exports.
• US demand, although buoyant, continues to track sideways. The imposition of
a slightly higher tariff on New Zealand exports by the US could well result in a

softening in demand in that market for New Zealand lamb.
• Ongoing economic weakness in China is encouraging some consumers to trade
down to cheaper cuts which could limit further price growth.

Demand for quality is helping to keep prices at firm levels.
• Strong global demand and growth in premium varieties are expected to keep
kiwifruit prices firm over the next year, supporting grower returns at around

current strong levels.
• Rising sales of SunGold and RubyRed kiwifruit should help sustain high per-
hectare returns and profitability for New Zealand growers despite larger
crop volumes.
• That said, higher freight costs, pricing pressure in China, and geopolitical
uncertainty may limit further price increases, but grower returns are still
expected to remain favourable.
• Strong demand from Asia, North America and other premium markets is
expected to keep apple prices firm to slightly higher over the next year,

supporting grower revenues and orchard profitability.
• Limited supplies of premium apple varieties should maintain price premiums,
benefiting growers with newer, higher-value cultivars and strong export
market positions.
• Improved market access, including India, and a shift toward higher-value
cultivars are likely to provide further support for prices, although a larger global
crop could moderate gains.

Limited upside for log prices for the foreseeable future.
• Forestry prices are expected to strengthen modestly through the second half of
2026 as market conditions improve.
• On the supply side, lower harvesting volumes and the wind-down of salvage

logging following Cyclone Gabrielle and other storms are expected to tighten log
supply and support prices. Elevated freight and energy costs, combined with a
still-soft New Zealand dollar, are likely to weigh on returns.
• Demand conditions are improving, with domestic timber demand supported by
a stronger residential construction outlook and rising building consents.
• Meanwhile, the NZ-India Free Trade Agreement has improved the
competitiveness of New Zealand logs and timber in the fast-growing, albeit

still relatively small, Indian market. This should support a recovery in exports
following the disruption caused by the 2020 biohazard dispute.
• Despite these gains, China remains the key driver of forestry markets. A recovery
in Chinese construction activity would boost log demand. Ongoing weakness in
the property sector is, however, likely to constrain price growth.
• Overall, forestry prices should remain stable to moderately firmer over the
next 12 months, supported by tighter supply and improving demand, although

weakness in China's property market is likely to limit significant upside.
